+  var MEDIA_MAX_W = 1920, MEDIA_MAX_H = 1080;
+
+  // compressImage(file) -> Promise<File>
+  //
+  // Downscales to at most 1920x1080 (preserving aspect ratio; smaller images are left at their
+  // original size) and re-encodes as JPEG at quality 0.8 BEFORE the file ever reaches
+  // uploadedMedia / the export POST. A phone photo can be tens of MB; MAX_BODY_BYTES (the
+  // server's export payload cap) is 2MB, and shrinking client-side avoids having to relax that
+  // protection limit just to let one big picture through.
+  function compressImage(file) {
+    return new Promise(function (resolve, reject) {
+      var url = URL.createObjectURL(file);
+      var img = new Image();
+      img.onload = function () {
+        URL.revokeObjectURL(url);
+        var w = img.naturalWidth, h = img.naturalHeight;
+        if (w > MEDIA_MAX_W || h > MEDIA_MAX_H) {
+          var scale = Math.min(MEDIA_MAX_W / w, MEDIA_MAX_H / h);
+          w = Math.max(1, Math.round(w * scale));
+          h = Math.max(1, Math.round(h * scale));
+        }
+        var canvas = document.createElement('canvas');
+        canvas.width = w;
+        canvas.height = h;
+        canvas.getContext('2d').drawImage(img, 0, 0, w, h);
+        canvas.toBlob(function (blob) {
+          if (!blob) { resolve(file); return; } // toBlob declined (exotic source format) -- ship the original rather than fail the insert
+          var dot = file.name ? file.name.lastIndexOf('.') : -1;
+          var stem = dot > 0 ? file.name.slice(0, dot) : (file.name || 'image');
+          resolve(new File([blob], stem + '.jpg', { type: 'image/jpeg' }));
+        }, 'image/jpeg', 0.8);
+      };
+      // Not decodable as an image (corrupt file, unsupported codec): fall back to the original so
+      // insertMedia's own handling of it -- not this function's -- decides what happens next.
+      img.onerror = function () { URL.revokeObjectURL(url); resolve(file); };
+      img.src = url;
+    });
+  }
+
   function mediaInputHandler(type) {
     return function () {
       var file = this.files && this.files[0];
       this.value = ''; // clears the input so picking the SAME file again still fires `change`
       if (!file) return;
-      insertMedia(type, file);
+      if (type === 'image') {
+        compressImage(file).then(function (compressed) { insertMedia(type, compressed); });
+      } else {
+        insertMedia(type, file);
+      }
     };
   }

tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\body_limit_brief.md-10-# Task Description
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\body_limit_brief.md-11-The user encountered an HTTP 413 "payload exceeds 2097152 bytes" error when exporting a deck with images.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\body_limit_brief.md:12:Currently, `MAX_BODY_BYTES` in `app.py` is set to `2 * 1024 * 1024` (2MB). However, now that images are embedded as Base64 strings in the JSON payload, a deck with multiple images can easily exceed this limit. `MAX_IMAGES` is 4 and `MAX_IMAGE_BYTES` is 1.4MB, meaning a maximum valid payload with 4 images could be up to ~7.5MB in Base64 encoding.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\body_limit_brief.md-13-
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\body_limit_brief.md:14:Please increase `MAX_BODY_BYTES` to 10MB (`10 * 1024 * 1024`) to accommodate decks with images.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\body_limit_brief.md-15-
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\body_limit_brief.md-16-## Instructions
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\body_limit_brief.md-17-1. Open `tasks/AI_Powered_PPT/src/aippt/canvas/app.py`.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\body_limit_brief.md:18:2. Locate `MAX_BODY_BYTES = 2 * 1024 * 1024`.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\body_limit_brief.md-19-3. Update the value to `10 * 1024 * 1024`.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\body_limit_brief.md-20-4. Update the preceding comment to explain that 10MB is needed to accommodate up to 4 Base64-encoded images.

tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\3D Logic 개발 테스트벤치 설계안_Ver2.html-367-        <ul>

tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\3D Logic 개발 테스트벤치 설계안_Ver2.html-369-            <li><strong>케이블 하네스 저비용 재제작:</strong> 복잡한 산업용 커넥터 대신, 테스트벤치 전용 터미널 블록 및 플렉시블 가이드 튜브를 활용하여 자체 하네스 배선 공수 절감.</li>

tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\image_compress_brief.md-10-# Task Description
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\image_compress_brief.md:11:The user wants to compress and resize imported images on the client side before they are stored or exported. This prevents large images from exhausting the 2MB HTTP payload limit (`MAX_BODY_BYTES`) during export to PPTX, while avoiding the need to relax the server's protection limits.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\image_compress_brief.md-12-
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\image_compress_brief.md-13-## Instructions
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\image_compress_brief.md-14-1. Open `tasks/AI_Powered_PPT/src/aippt/canvas/static/editor.js`.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\image_compress_brief.md-15-2. Implement a new asynchronous function `compressImage(file)` that takes an `image/*` File object.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\image_compress_brief.md-16-   - It should read the file into an `Image` object.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\image_compress_brief.md-17-   - It should create an off-screen `<canvas>`.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\image_compress_brief.md-18-   - If the image exceeds `1920x1080` in either dimension, calculate a scaled-down size while preserving aspect ratio. Otherwise, use the original dimensions.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\image_compress_brief.md-19-   - Draw the image onto the canvas at the calculated size.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\image_compress_brief.md:20:   - Call `canvas.toBlob` with `image/jpeg` and a quality of `0.8`.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\image_compress_brief.md-21-   - Return the resulting Blob as a new `File` object (give it a `.jpg` extension if needed, or just return the Blob to be registered).
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\image_compress_brief.md:22:3. Modify the existing `mediaInputHandler(type)` function:
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\image_compress_brief.md-23-   - If `type === 'image'`, instead of calling `insertMedia(type, file)` directly, pass the file to `compressImage(file)`.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\image_compress_brief.md-24-   - Await the compressed file (or use `.then()`), and then call `insertMedia(type, compressedFile)`.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\image_compress_brief.md-25-   - If `type === 'video'`, do not compress it; just call `insertMedia(type, file)` directly.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\image_compress_brief.md:26:4. Ensure the UI still shows "Added image X." properly and that no functionality is broken. Remember `mediaInputHandler` is bound to the `<input type="file">` change event, so handling the async flow cleanly is important.

tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_work_order.md-14-### 1. Media Files (Image/Video) Support
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_work_order.md-15-- **UI**: Add "Add Image" and "Add Video" buttons to the sidebar toolbar. When clicked, trigger a file input (`<input type="file" accept="image/*">` etc.).
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_work_order.md:16:- **State**: When a user selects a file, read the file and store the `File` object in a global map (e.g., `window.uploadedMedia = window.uploadedMedia || {}; uploadedMedia[file.name] = file;`). 
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_work_order.md-17-- **DOM**: Add a new element to the `ir.slides[activeSlideIndex].elements` array with `type: "image"` or `"video"`, and `src: file.name`. Render an `<img>` or `<video>` tag inside the stage so it can be resized/moved like text boxes. (Use `URL.createObjectURL(file)` as the DOM `src`).
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_work_order.md:18:- **Export**: Modify `exportPPT()` in `editor.js`. Change the fetch call to `/api/download`. Instead of sending JSON, use `FormData`. `formData.append("ir", JSON.stringify(payload))`. Then loop through `uploadedMedia` and `formData.append(filename, fileObj)`. Remove `application/json` headers so the browser sets the multipart boundary automatically.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_work_order.md-19-
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_work_order.md-20-### 2. Multi-selection & Alignment
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_work_order.md-21-- **Selection**: Allow Shift+Click in `editor.js` / `interactions.js` to select multiple elements. Keep track of them (e.g., `window.selectedElementIds = [...]`).
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_work_order.md-22-- **UI**: Add Alignment buttons in the properties panel: Align Top, Bottom, Left, Right, Center X, Center Y.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_work_order.md-23-- **Logic**: When alignment buttons are clicked, find all selected elements, determine the bounding box (e.g. min Y for Top align), and update their `x` or `y` values.

tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md:96:  `'0deg'`) and lazily binds `<img>.src`/`<video>.src` from `window.uploadedMedia[s.src].url`, only
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-97-  when the bound src actually changed (`media.dataset.boundSrc`) — `renderEl()` runs on every commit
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-98-  (every drag/resize frame), and re-assigning `.src` every frame would reload the image / restart the
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-99-  video continuously.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-100-- `toIR()`: a third branch emits `{id, type, x, y, w, h, src}` only — **no `fill`, no `stroke`, no
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-101-  `text`, no `rotation`**. This is a deliberate deviation from the brief's literal read (which does
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-102-  not mention media's fill/stroke/rotation at all, but the general shape-serialization code the
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-103-  brief points at — `exportPPT()`'s neighbourhood — would have inherited them from copy-paste). The
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-104-  reason: `render_canvas.draw_image()`/`draw_video()` never draw fill/stroke/rotation for a picture
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-105-  or a video, so emitting them would be schema-legal (both are optional properties on `image`/`video`
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-106-  per `schema.json`) but silently inert — a control or a field that does nothing when you use it.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-107-  The property panel accordingly never offers those controls for a media selection (see `syncProps()`
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-108-  below), so this editor's *state* never carries them for media in the first place; there was nothing
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-109-  to strip at serialization.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-110-- `stateFromIR()` (lifted, Gate 4b): a new early-return branch, checked by literal string
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-111-  (`e.type === 'image' || e.type === 'video'`, not `isMediaType()` — see the ReferenceError note
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-112-  above), returning `{id, type, x, y, w, h, rot, src}`. Never throws, never repairs, matching the
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-113-  existing contract for every other branch.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md:114:- Media insertion (`registerMedia()`, `insertMedia()`, `mediaInputHandler()`, wired to
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-115-  `#b-add-image`/`#b-add-video`/`#f-image`/`#f-video`): reads the picked `File`, registers it in
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md:116:  `window.uploadedMedia` (a session-scoped `{name: {file, url}}` map — see caveat 4 on the deliberate
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md:117:  deviation from the brief's literal `uploadedMedia[file.name] = file`), and drops a
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-118-  `MEDIA_DEFAULT_SIZE` box centred in the canvas through `applyConstraints()` — the SAME judge every
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-119-  other creation path goes through, so an image/video cannot land on a reserved region any more than
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-120-  a rect can.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-121-- Export (`#b-export`'s handler): the fetch target changed from `/api/export` (JSON body) to
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-122-  `/api/download` (multipart `FormData`: an `"ir"` field with the same `JSON.stringify(serialize())`
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-123-  this editor always produced, plus one form part per distinct image/video `src` the current deck
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md:124:  references, read from `window.uploadedMedia`). **No `Content-Type` header is set** — `fetch()`
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-125-  computes the multipart boundary itself from the `FormData` body; a hand-set header would carry no
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-126-  boundary and the server would fail to parse the parts, per the brief's own instruction. A
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md:127:  pre-flight check (`mediaFor(ir)` + a `window.uploadedMedia` lookup) warns and refuses to POST if any
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-128-  referenced media file's bytes are no longer in this tab (e.g. after a reload, or a loaded/pasted
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-129-  deck naming a file nothing here was ever uploaded for) — added because `app.py`'s
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-130-  `draw_image()`/`draw_video()` raise a bare `ValueError` for a `src` with no matching upload, which
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-131-  `app.py` does not map to a 4xx (confirmed by reading `app.py`'s except clauses:
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-132-  `StalePayloadError`/`CanvasValidationError`/`TemplatePinError` only), so an unmapped exception would
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-133-  otherwise surface as an opaque 500 after a real round trip instead of an immediate, actionable
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-134-  client-side warning.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-135-- The brief's exact function/variable names (`exportPPT()`, `ir.slides[activeSlideIndex].elements`)
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-136-  do not exist in this codebase — there is one slide, one `stage`, and the export handler is an
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-137-  anonymous `click` listener on `#b-export`. I modified that handler in place rather than inventing
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-138-  a mismatched `exportPPT()` function.

tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-273-   (`'shape' | 'connector' | 'media'`), and `data-for` now accepts a space-separated list of
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-274-   categories** (e.g. `data-for="shape connector"` on the Line group) rather than a single value.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-275-   This was necessary because a THIRD category (media) needed to hide from Fill/Text/Rotation (like a
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-276-   connector already did) while ALSO hiding from the previously-unscoped Line section (which a
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-277-   connector legitimately needs, for its line colour). `#align-group` and `#arrange-group` are driven
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-278-   directly (not through the `data-for` loop) because they must show/hide based on
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-279-   `selectionSet().length`/`!!selected` respectively, independent of — and in `#align-group`'s case,
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-280-   even when there is no — a single selected element's *type*.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-281-
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md:282:4. **`window.uploadedMedia` stores `{file: File, url: objectURL}`, not a bare `File`** as the brief's
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md:283:   literal example (`uploadedMedia[file.name] = file`) shows, and `registerMedia()` de-duplicates by
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-284-   filename (`photo.jpg`, `photo-1.jpg`, ...) rather than letting a second same-named upload silently
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-285-   shadow the first. Two reasons: (a) `renderEl()` runs on every commit (every drag/resize frame), so
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-286-   calling `URL.createObjectURL(file)` fresh each render — which the brief's literal text suggests —
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-287-   would leak a new blob URL every frame instead of creating and caching exactly one per upload; (b)
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-288-   without de-duplication, uploading a second file that happens to share a name with an earlier one
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-289-   would overwrite the map entry, and any element still pointing at the first upload's `src` would
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-290-   silently start rendering — and exporting — the second file's bytes instead.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-291-
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-292-5. **Multi-selection is additive to `selected`, not a rewrite of the single-selection model into an
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-293-   N-element one**, and it drives alignment only — not multi-drag, multi-delete, multi-duplicate, or
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-294-   multi-property-edit. Every other mutation in this file (drag, resize, rotate, the property panel,
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_frontend_change_detail.md-295-   delete, duplicate, undo-per-gesture) is built for exactly one active element; rearchitecting all of

tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_backend_change_detail.md-146-
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_backend_change_detail.md-147-3. **No post-parse total-size check on the multipart body**, unlike `_read_json()`'s two-layer check
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_backend_change_detail.md-148-   for JSON (`Content-Length` header, then actual buffered length). Starlette's `Request.form()`
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_backend_change_detail.md-149-   parses and spools multipart parts internally (large parts go to temp files automatically) before
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_backend_change_detail.md-150-   this code sees any of it, so there is no single "raw bytes read so far" value to compare against
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_backend_change_detail.md:151:   `MAX_BODY_BYTES` the way the JSON path does. The `Content-Length` header check still runs up
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_backend_change_detail.md-152-   front and rejects any request that *declares* an oversized body; a chunked request that lies about
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_backend_change_detail.md-153-   its length (declares small, sends large) would not be caught until `python-multipart` (or memory)
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_backend_change_detail.md-154-   protests. Adding a manual streaming byte-counter around `request.form()` was judged more machinery
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_backend_change_detail.md-155-   than this brief asked for; noting it here as a known gap rather than silently shipping it.
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_backend_change_detail.md-156-
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_backend_change_detail.md-157-4. **`python-multipart` is required for `Request.form()` and is not currently declared as a
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_backend_change_detail.md-158-   dependency.** `pyproject.toml`'s `[editor]` extra lists only `fastapi`/`uvicorn`; FastAPI's
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_backend_change_detail.md-159-   multipart form parsing needs the separate `python-multipart` package installed, or
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_backend_change_detail.md-160-   `await request.form()` raises at request time. `pyproject.toml` is not in this brief's
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_backend_change_detail.md-161-   `write_scope`, so it was not edited — flagging so a future phase (or whoever wires up
tasks/AI_Powered_PPT\phase6_backend_change_detail.md-162-   `[editor]`) adds `python-multipart` to it.

  /* [REDACTED] media insertion (Phase 6) --
   *
   * Not a drag-out tool: a picture/video comes from a file picker, so it is placed centred in the
   * body-safe area at a fixed default size and immediately selected, the way a click-to-drop
   * shape is. window.uploadedMedia is the SESSION's own file store -- {name: {file, url}} -- named
   * on `window` rather than a closure-local var because the export handler (below) needs to read
   * the exact same File objects to build the multipart body it POSTs.
   */
  window.uploadedMedia = window.uploadedMedia || {};

  // Registers `file` under its own name, or a de-duplicated variant of it when that name already
  // names a DIFFERENT File -- two uploads both called "photo.jpg" must not collide, or the second
  // would silently shadow the first and an element still pointing at the first's `src` would
  // export whichever file happened to win the race. Returns the src to store on the element.
  function registerMedia(file) {
    var name = file.name || 'media';
    var n = 1;
    while (window.uploadedMedia[name] && window.uploadedMedia[name].file !== file) {
      var dot = file.name.lastIndexOf('.');
      var stem = dot > 0 ? file.name.slice(0, dot) : file.name;
      var ext = dot > 0 ? file.name.slice(dot) : '';
      name = stem + '-' + (n++) + ext;
    }
    if (!window.uploadedMedia[name]) {
      window.uploadedMedia[name] = { file: file, url: URL.createObjectURL(file) };
    }
    return name;
  }

  function insertMedia(type, file) {
    var src = registerMedia(file);
    var sz = MEDIA_DEFAULT_SIZE;
    var cand = {
      id: nextId(), type: type,
      x: (CANVAS.w - sz.w) / 2, y: (CANVAS.h - sz.h) / 2, w: sz.w, h: sz.h, rot: 0,
      src: src,
    };
    // Goes through the SAME judge as everything else on the stage (alt: true -- a deliberate
    // placement is not re-snapped, but is still clamped on-slide, exactly like click-to-drop).
    var legal = applyConstraints(cand, { alt: true });
    if (!legal) { setStatus('That ' + type + ' would not fit legally here ??nothing was added.'); return; }
    var before = snapshot();
    var made = addEl(makeEl(legal));
    if (multi.length) clearMulti();
    select(made);
    pushUndo(before); // one step: the insertion and its placement are a single act
    setStatus('Added ' + type + ' ' + legal.id + '.');
  }

  var MEDIA_MAX_W = 1920, MEDIA_MAX_H = 1080;

  // compressImage(file) -> Promise<File>
  //
  // Downscales to at most 1920x1080 (preserving aspect ratio; smaller images are left at their
  // original size) and re-encodes as JPEG at quality 0.8 BEFORE the file ever reaches
  // uploadedMedia / the export POST. A phone photo can be tens of MB; MAX_BODY_BYTES (the
  // server's export payload cap) is 2MB, and shrinking client-side avoids having to relax that
  // protection limit just to let one big picture through.
  function compressImage(file) {
    return new Promise(function (resolve, reject) {
      var url = URL.createObjectURL(file);
      var img = new Image();
      img.onload = function () {
        URL.revokeObjectURL(url);
        var w = img.naturalWidth, h = img.naturalHeight;
        if (w > MEDIA_MAX_W || h > MEDIA_MAX_H) {
          var scale = Math.min(MEDIA_MAX_W / w, MEDIA_MAX_H / h);
          w = Math.max(1, Math.round(w * scale));
          h = Math.max(1, Math.round(h * scale));
        }
        var canvas = document.createElement('canvas');
        canvas.width = w;
        canvas.height = h;
        canvas.getContext('2d').drawImage(img, 0, 0, w, h);
        canvas.toBlob(function (blob) {
          if (!blob) { resolve(file); return; } // toBlob declined (exotic source format) -- ship the original rather than fail the insert
          var dot = file.name ? file.name.lastIndexOf('.') : -1;
          var stem = dot > 0 ? file.name.slice(0, dot) : (file.name || 'image');
          resolve(new File([blob], stem + '.jpg', { type: 'image/jpeg' }));
        }, 'image/jpeg', 0.8);
      };
      // Not decodable as an image (corrupt file, unsupported codec): fall back to the original so
      // insertMedia's own handling of it -- not this function's -- decides what happens next.
      img.onerror = function () { URL.revokeObjectURL(url); resolve(file); };
      img.src = url;
    });
  }

  function mediaInputHandler(type) {
    return function () {
      var file = this.files && this.files[0];
      this.value = ''; // clears the input so picking the SAME file again still fires `change`
      if (!file) return;
      if (type === 'image') {
        compressImage(file).then(function (compressed) { insertMedia(type, compressed); });
      } else {
        insertMedia(type, file);
      }
    };
  }
  // Wired further down, in "the toolbar" section -- $() (document.getElementById) is not defined
  // until then, and these are plain statements that run at script-load time, not inside a function.

    if (tool === 'select' && !ev.target.closest('.el') && !ev.target.closest('#title-field')) {
      if (multi.length) clearMulti();
      select(null);
    }
  });

  /* [REDACTED] media insertion (Phase 6) --
   *
   * Not a drag-out tool: a picture/video comes from a file picker, so it is placed centred in the
   * body-safe area at a fixed default size and immediately selected, the way a click-to-drop
   * shape is. window.uploadedMedia is the SESSION's own file store -- {name: {file, url}} -- named
   * on `window` rather than a closure-local var because the export handler (below) needs to read
   * the exact same File objects to build the multipart body it POSTs.
   */
  window.uploadedMedia = window.uploadedMedia || {};

  // Registers `file` under its own name, or a de-duplicated variant of it when that name already
  // names a DIFFERENT File -- two uploads both called "photo.jpg" must not collide, or the second
  // would silently shadow the first and an element still pointing at the first's `src` would
  // export whichever file happened to win the race. Returns the src to store on the element.
  function registerMedia(file) {
    var name = file.name || 'media';
    var n = 1;
    while (window.uploadedMedia[name] && window.uploadedMedia[name].file !== file) {
      var dot = file.name.lastIndexOf('.');
      var stem = dot > 0 ? file.name.slice(0, dot) : file.name;
      var ext = dot > 0 ? file.name.slice(dot) : '';
      name = stem + '-' + (n++) + ext;
    }
    if (!window.uploadedMedia[name]) {
      window.uploadedMedia[name] = { file: file, url: URL.createObjectURL(file) };
    }
    return name;
  }

  function insertMedia(type, file) {
    var src = registerMedia(file);
    var sz = MEDIA_DEFAULT_SIZE;
    var cand = {
      id: nextId(), type: type,
      x: (CANVAS.w - sz.w) / 2, y: (CANVAS.h - sz.h) / 2, w: sz.w, h: sz.h, rot: 0,
      src: src,
    };
    // Goes through the SAME judge as everything else on the stage (alt: true -- a deliberate
    // placement is not re-snapped, but is still clamped on-slide, exactly like click-to-drop).
    var legal = applyConstraints(cand, { alt: true });
    if (!legal) { setStatus('That ' + type + ' would not fit legally here ??nothing was added.'); return; }
    var before = snapshot();
    var made = addEl(makeEl(legal));
    if (multi.length) clearMulti();
    select(made);
    pushUndo(before); // one step: the insertion and its placement are a single act
    setStatus('Added ' + type + ' ' + legal.id + '.');
  }

  var MEDIA_MAX_W = 1920, MEDIA_MAX_H = 1080;

  // compressImage(file) -> Promise<File>
  //
  // Downscales to at most 1920x1080 (preserving aspect ratio; smaller images are left at their
  // original size) and re-encodes as JPEG at quality 0.8 BEFORE the file ever reaches
  // uploadedMedia / the export POST. A phone photo can be tens of MB; MAX_BODY_BYTES (the
  // server's export payload cap) is 2MB, and shrinking client-side avoids having to relax that
  // protection limit just to let one big picture through.
  function compressImage(file) {
    return new Promise(function (resolve, reject) {
      var url = URL.createObjectURL(file);
      var img = new Image();
      img.onload = function () {
        URL.revokeObjectURL(url);
        var w = img.naturalWidth, h = img.naturalHeight;
        if (w > MEDIA_MAX_W || h > MEDIA_MAX_H) {
          var scale = Math.min(MEDIA_MAX_W / w, MEDIA_MAX_H / h);
          w = Math.max(1, Math.round(w * scale));
          h = Math.max(1, Math.round(h * scale));
        }
        var canvas = document.createElement('canvas');
        canvas.width = w;
        canvas.height = h;
        canvas.getContext('2d').drawImage(img, 0, 0, w, h);
        canvas.toBlob(function (blob) {
          if (!blob) { resolve(file); return; } // toBlob declined (exotic source format) -- ship the original rather than fail the insert
          var dot = file.name ? file.name.lastIndexOf('.') : -1;
          var stem = dot > 0 ? file.name.slice(0, dot) : (file.name || 'image');
          resolve(new File([blob], stem + '.jpg', { type: 'image/jpeg' }));
        }, 'image/jpeg', 0.8);
      };
      // Not decodable as an image (corrupt file, unsupported codec): fall back to the original so
      // insertMedia's own handling of it -- not this function's -- decides what happens next.
      img.onerror = function () { URL.revokeObjectURL(url); resolve(file); };
      img.src = url;
    });
  }

  function mediaInputHandler(type) {
    return function () {
      var file = this.files && this.files[0];
      this.value = ''; // clears the input so picking the SAME file again still fires `change`
      if (!file) return;
      if (type === 'image') {
        compressImage(file).then(function (compressed) { insertMedia(type, compressed); });
      } else {
        insertMedia(type, file);
      }
    };
  }
  // Wired further down, in "the toolbar" section -- $() (document.getElementById) is not defined
  // until then, and these are plain statements that run at script-load time, not inside a function.

  stage.addEventListener('pointerdown', function (ev) {
    if (tool === 'select' && !ev.target.closest('.el') && !ev.target.closest('#title-field')) {
      if (multi.length) clearMulti();
      select(null);
    }
  });

# Task Description
The user wants to compress and resize imported images on the client side before they are stored or exported. This prevents large images from exhausting the 2MB HTTP payload limit (`MAX_BODY_BYTES`) during export to PPTX, while avoiding the need to relax the server's protection limits.

## Instructions
1. Open `tasks/AI_Powered_PPT/src/aippt/canvas/static/editor.js`.
2. Implement a new asynchronous function `compressImage(file)` that takes an `image/*` File object.
   - It should read the file into an `Image` object.
   - It should create an off-screen `<canvas>`.
   - If the image exceeds `1920x1080` in either dimension, calculate a scaled-down size while preserving aspect ratio. Otherwise, use the original dimensions.
   - Draw the image onto the canvas at the calculated size.
   - Call `canvas.toBlob` with `image/jpeg` and a quality of `0.8`.
   - Return the resulting Blob as a new `File` object (give it a `.jpg` extension if needed, or just return the Blob to be registered).
3. Modify the existing `mediaInputHandler(type)` function:
   - If `type === 'image'`, instead of calling `insertMedia(type, file)` directly, pass the file to `compressImage(file)`.
   - Await the compressed file (or use `.then()`), and then call `insertMedia(type, compressedFile)`.
   - If `type === 'video'`, do not compress it; just call `insertMedia(type, file)` directly.
4. Ensure the UI still shows "Added image X." properly and that no functionality is broken. Remember `mediaInputHandler` is bound to the `<input type="file">` change event, so handling the async flow cleanly is important.

Document your changes in `tasks/AI_Powered_PPT/image_compress_change_detail.md`.

# Change Detail: Client-side image compression before insert

## File changed
`tasks/AI_Powered_PPT/src/aippt/canvas/static/editor.js`

## Why
Imported images (e.g. phone photos) can be tens of MB. The export endpoint enforces
`MAX_BODY_BYTES` (2MB) on the multipart POST it builds from `window.uploadedMedia`. Compressing
and downscaling images client-side, before they are registered/stored, keeps large images from
blowing that budget without relaxing the server's protection limit.

## What changed

1. Added `compressImage(file)` (new function, placed between `insertMedia` and
   `mediaInputHandler`, both in the "media insertion (Phase 6)" section):
   - Loads the given `image/*` File into an `Image` via an object URL
     (`URL.createObjectURL`/`URL.revokeObjectURL`), consistent with how `registerMedia` already
     handles File -> URL for the live preview.
   - If either dimension exceeds `1920x1080` (`MEDIA_MAX_W`/`MEDIA_MAX_H`), computes a
     uniform-scale target size that preserves aspect ratio and fits within that box; otherwise
     keeps the original dimensions.
   - Draws the image onto an off-screen `<canvas>` at the target size.
   - Encodes via `canvas.toBlob(..., 'image/jpeg', 0.8)` and resolves a new `File` (same stem,
     `.jpg` extension, `image/jpeg` type) built from the resulting Blob.
   - Returns a Promise; resolves the *original, uncompressed* `file` as a fallback if the image
     fails to decode (`img.onerror`) or `toBlob` yields no Blob, so a bad/exotic input still
     reaches `insertMedia` (and its existing constraint/status handling) instead of silently
     vanishing.

2. Modified `mediaInputHandler(type)`:
   - `type === 'video'`: unchanged, calls `insertMedia(type, file)` directly (no compression).
   - `type === 'image'`: now calls `compressImage(file).then(function (compressed) {
     insertMedia(type, compressed); })` instead of calling `insertMedia` directly.
   - The input's `change` handler was already effectively async in spirit (it clears
     `this.value` synchronously up front, before any of this runs), so deferring the
     `insertMedia` call into the `.then()` callback does not change re-pick-same-file behavior or
     introduce any race with the input element itself.

## Behavior / UI impact
- `insertMedia` is unchanged, so the existing "Added image <id>." status message, undo-stack
  entry, constraint/placement logic (`applyConstraints`), and multi-select clearing all still run
  exactly as before -- just against the compressed File instead of the original.
- Videos are untouched (no compression, no re-encode, no extension change).
- A picture that is already <=1920x1080 is still re-encoded as JPEG at quality 0.8 (per the
  brief's instructions); this does re-encode e.g. small PNGs with transparency into JPEG, which
  flattens transparency onto a black background (canvas default) and drops it. No existing
  callers of `uploadedMedia`/`insertMedia` assume alpha is preserved, but this is a visible
  side-effect of following the brief literally (always JPEG, always quality 0.8) rather than
  only compressing images that were actually oversized.

## Assumptions / caveats
- The brief says "give it a `.jpg` extension if needed, or just return the Blob to be
  registered" -- implemented as always returning a `File` (not a bare Blob) named `<stem>.jpg`,
  since `registerMedia`/`insertMedia` read `file.name` for both display and de-dup naming, and a
  bare Blob has no `.name`.
- No explicit error surfaced to the user if compression fails outright; `compressImage` resolves
  the original file in that case so the existing (already-implemented) `insertMedia` constraint
  checks and status line are the only feedback path, same as before this change for any image.
- Did not add a `.jpg`-forcing rename when the ORIGINAL file already had a non-`.jpg` image
  extension (e.g. `.png`, `.webp`) beyond the stem-preserving rename described above -- confirmed
  this is exactly what "give it a `.jpg` extension if needed" asks for, not a content-type
  passthrough.
- Did not touch `approved_write_manifest`
  (`tasks/AI_Powered_PPT/image_compress_manifest.txt`) -- it did not exist prior to this change
  and the brief's Instructions section does not ask for it to be created.

VERDICT: WARNING

The diff is syntactically valid and correctly implements the requested asynchronous image compression flow without changing video handling.

Material caveats:

- A 1920×1080 JPEG at quality 0.8 can still exceed the effective ~1.5 MB binary budget after Base64 expansion, especially with multiple images. Thus, export can still breach the 2 MB request limit.
- Compression failure falls back to the original potentially oversized file, also leaving the limit unprotected.
- Converting every image to JPEG removes transparency, visibly affecting PNG/WebP images.
